# 7. Target Dependency per Module

## 7.1 `payment-domain`

Allowed:

```text
java.base
optional: small annotation-only dependency if justified
```

Suspicious:

```text
jakarta.ws.rs
org.glassfish.jersey.*
org.mybatis
org.postgresql
com.fasterxml.jackson.databind
software.amazon.awssdk.*
com.azure.*
```

Check:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-domain dependency:tree
```

Expected ideal:

```text
Very small dependency tree.
```

## 7.2 `payment-api`

Allowed:

```text
jakarta.validation, if DTO validation annotations are used
JSON annotation dependency, if contract intentionally uses it
```

Suspicious:

```text
org.mybatis
org.postgresql
jersey-server implementation
database migration libraries
cloud SDK
```

## 7.3 `payment-application`

Allowed:

```text
payment-domain
possibly application-level shared abstractions
```

Suspicious:

```text
jakarta.ws.rs
org.mybatis
org.postgresql
jersey-server
jackson-databind, unless use case intentionally processes JSON
```

## 7.4 `payment-rest`

Allowed:

```text
payment-api
payment-application
payment-domain exceptions/model if needed
jakarta.ws.rs-api
Jersey API/runtime depending layout
JSON provider integration
validation integration
```

Suspicious:

```text
payment-persistence-mybatis
org.postgresql
migration tool
direct database utility
```

## 7.5 `payment-persistence-mybatis`

Allowed:

```text
payment-domain
mybatis
postgresql driver at runtime
java.sql
connection pool if owned here
```

Suspicious:

```text
jakarta.ws.rs
payment-rest
Jersey server
API DTO, unless intentionally mapping persistence directly to API contract, which is usually bad
```

## 7.6 `payment-bootstrap`

Allowed:

```text
payment-rest
payment-application
payment-domain
payment-persistence-mybatis
integration adapters
Jersey runtime
database setup
configuration libraries
```

Bootstrap is the composition root, so it may legitimately know many implementation modules.

---

# 8. Maven Dependency Analyze

## 8.1 What It Does

Maven Dependency Plugin includes dependency analysis goals that determine categories such as used-and-declared, used-and-undeclared, and unused-and-declared dependencies. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/plugins/maven-dependency-plugin/plugin-info.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Run:

```bash
mvn dependency:analyze
```

Per module:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:analyze
mvn -pl payment-application dependency:analyze
```

For CI lifecycle usage:

```bash
mvn test-compile dependency:analyze-only
```

The plugin documentation notes `dependency:analyze-only` is intended for build lifecycle use and assumes `test-compile` has already executed. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/plugins/maven-dependency-plugin/usage.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 8.2 How to Interpret

Potential issue:

```text
Used undeclared dependencies found:
   com.company.payment:payment-domain
```

Meaning:

```text
Code imports payment-domain types, but POM did not directly declare dependency.
It may be available transitively through payment-application.
```

This is risky because compile success depends on transitive dependency leakage.

Fix:

```xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>com.company.payment</groupId>
    <artifactId>payment-domain</artifactId>
    <version>${project.version}</version>
</dependency>
```

But do not blindly add it. Ask first:

```text
Should this module really use domain directly?
Or should it go through application/API?
```

Potential issue:

```text
Unused declared dependencies found:
   org.postgresql:postgresql
```

If in `payment-rest`, likely remove it. If in `payment-bootstrap` and loaded reflectively, it may be a false positive. Dependency analysis is bytecode-based and may miss reflection/config/resource-driven usage, so review is still required.

---

# 9. Dependency Convergence

## 9.1 Problem

Example:

```text
payment-bootstrap
├── jersey-json-jackson → jackson-databind 2.17.x
└── another-library     → jackson-databind 2.15.x
```

Maven may resolve one version based on dependency mediation. If runtime expects a method from the newer version but older version is loaded, symptoms can include:

```text
NoSuchMethodError
NoClassDefFoundError
ClassCastException
serialization behavior mismatch
```

## 9.2 Maven Enforcer: dependencyConvergence

Maven Enforcer’s `dependencyConvergence` rule requires dependency versions to be the same everywhere in the tree; if two dependencies bring different versions of the same artifact, the rule can fail the build. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/enforcer/enforcer-rules/dependencyConvergence.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example:

```xml
<plugin>
    <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>
    <artifactId>maven-enforcer-plugin</artifactId>
    <version>${maven.enforcer.plugin.version}</version>
    <executions>
        <execution>
            <id>enforce-dependency-convergence</id>
            <goals>
                <goal>enforce</goal>
            </goals>
            <configuration>
                <rules>
                    <dependencyConvergence/>
                </rules>
            </configuration>
        </execution>
    </executions>
</plugin>
```

Practical rule:

```text
If convergence fails, do not immediately exclude randomly.
First inspect both paths.
Then decide whether to align using dependencyManagement/BOM or exclude one transitive path.
```

---

# 10. Require Upper Bound Dependencies

## 10.1 Problem

Maven’s nearest-wins resolution can choose a lower version than another transitive dependency expects.

Example:

```text
A → C:1.0
B → D → C:2.0

Resolved C might become 1.0 depending graph distance.
```

## 10.2 Enforcer Rule

`requireUpperBoundDeps` requires the resolved dependency version to be equal to or higher than the highest version found in transitive dependencies. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/enforcer/enforcer-rules/requireUpperBoundDeps.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example:

```xml
<requireUpperBoundDeps/>
```

Use with care because it can be noisy in large enterprise builds. For service-level Maven projects, it is often valuable after dependency ownership is clean.

---

# 11. Banned Dependencies

## 11.1 Use Cases

Banned dependency rule is useful for preventing dependency regressions.

Examples:

```text
1. Ban javax.ws.rs after migration to jakarta.ws.rs.
2. Ban old logging bridges.
3. Ban vulnerable legacy libraries.
4. Ban embedded servlet container if service must run on specific runtime.
5. Ban internal libraries not allowed in this bounded context.
```

Maven Enforcer’s `bannedDependencies` rule checks dependencies and fails if matching excluded artifacts are found. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/enforcer/enforcer-rules/bannedDependencies.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 11.2 Example: Ban `javax.ws.rs`

```xml
<bannedDependencies>
    <excludes>
        <exclude>javax.ws.rs:javax.ws.rs-api</exclude>
    </excludes>
    <searchTransitive>true</searchTransitive>
</bannedDependencies>
```

Why useful:

```text
Jakarta migration can silently regress if old javax artifacts enter transitively.
```

## 11.3 Example: Ban REST → Persistence

Maven Enforcer can ban artifacts, but enforcing “module A must not depend on module B” is often easier using a custom script over `dependency:tree -DoutputType=json`.

Rule:

```text
payment-rest must not contain payment-persistence-mybatis
payment-domain must not contain jakarta.ws.rs
payment-application must not contain org.mybatis
```

---

# 12. Duplicate Classes

## 12.1 Problem

Duplicate class means the same fully qualified class exists in more than one dependency. This can create class shadowing and unpredictable behavior depending packaging/classpath order.

MojoHaus Extra Enforcer Rules includes `banDuplicateClasses`, which checks dependencies and fails if any class is present in more than one dependency. ([MojoHaus](https://www.mojohaus.org/extra-enforcer-rules/banDuplicateClasses.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 12.2 Example Plugin Config

```xml
<plugin>
    <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>
    <artifactId>maven-enforcer-plugin</artifactId>
    <version>${maven.enforcer.plugin.version}</version>
    <dependencies>
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.codehaus.mojo</groupId>
            <artifactId>extra-enforcer-rules</artifactId>
            <version>${extra.enforcer.rules.version}</version>
        </dependency>
    </dependencies>
    <executions>
        <execution>
            <id>ban-duplicate-classes</id>
            <goals>
                <goal>enforce</goal>
            </goals>
            <configuration>
                <rules>
                    <banDuplicateClasses>
                        <findAllDuplicates>true</findAllDuplicates>
                    </banDuplicateClasses>
                </rules>
            </configuration>
        </execution>
    </executions>
</plugin>
```

## 12.3 Common Causes

```text
1. Multiple logging implementations.
2. Old shaded libraries.
3. javax/jakarta mixed transition artifacts.
4. Vendor SDK bundling repackaged dependencies.
5. Fat JAR packaging pulling duplicate classes.
6. Test fixtures leaking into runtime scope.
```

## 12.4 Fix Strategy

```text
1. Identify both dependencies containing duplicate class.
2. Determine which one is canonical.
3. Exclude duplicate transitive dependency.
4. Align version through dependencyManagement/BOM.
5. Avoid ignoring unless class is known harmless and documented.
```

---

# 13. Split Package

## 13.1 What It Is

Split package happens when the same Java package is present in multiple JARs/modules.

Example:

```text
payment-domain.jar
└── com.company.payment.common.DateRange

payment-api.jar
└── com.company.payment.common.ErrorCode
```

This is especially problematic for JPMS because a package should have clear module ownership.

## 13.2 Why It Happens

```text
1. Generic package names: com.company.common
2. Copy-paste utility classes
3. Shared DTO package spread across modules
4. Multi-module migration without package rename
5. Generated sources using same package in multiple artifacts
```

## 13.3 Detection Script

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

mkdir -p target/dependency
mvn dependency:copy-dependencies \
  -DincludeScope=runtime \
  -DoutputDirectory=target/dependency

for jar in target/dependency/*.jar target/*.jar; do
  [ -f "$jar" ] || continue
  jar tf "$jar" \
    | grep '\.class$' \
    | sed 's|/[^/]*\.class$||' \
    | sed 's|/|.|g' \
    | sort -u \
    | awk -v jar="$jar" '{ print $0 " " jar }'
done \
| sort \
| awk '
{
  pkg=$1; jar=$2;
  jars[pkg]=jars[pkg] " " jar;
  count[pkg]++;
}
END {
  for (pkg in count) {
    if (count[pkg] > 1) {
      print pkg ":" jars[pkg];
    }
  }
}'
```

Use this as a first-pass check, then manually inspect. Some packages may appear in multi-release JARs or expected platform artifacts, so don’t blindly fail without tuning.

## 13.4 Fix Strategy

```text
Bad:
com.company.payment.common in multiple modules

Better:
com.company.payment.domain.common
com.company.payment.api.common
com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.common
```

Ownership rule:

```text
A package belongs to exactly one module.
If multiple modules need common types, extract a dedicated common-contract module with narrow scope.
```

---

# 14. Automatic Modules

## 14.1 What They Are

A non-modular JAR placed on the module path can become an automatic module. The dangerous part is that the module name may come from metadata or JAR naming, so guessing module names is risky.

Command:

```bash
jar --describe-module --file path/to/library.jar
```

For all runtime dependencies:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:copy-dependencies \
  -DincludeScope=runtime \
  -DoutputDirectory=target/dependency

for j in payment-bootstrap/target/dependency/*.jar; do
  echo "==== $j"
  jar --describe-module --file "$j" 2>/dev/null | head -30
done
```

## 14.2 What to Look For

Output may show:

```text
No module descriptor found. Derived automatic module.
```

Interpretation:

```text
This JAR is not a true explicit JPMS module.
It can work on module path, but its name and behavior need caution.
```

## 14.3 Practical Rule

```text
Do not write:
requires guessed.library.name;

Before:
jar --describe-module --file actual-library.jar
```

For framework libraries like Jersey, Jackson, MyBatis, validation provider, and cloud SDKs, validate the actual module name from the exact dependency version used by your project.

---

# 15. `jdeps` Analysis

## 15.1 Basic JAR Analysis

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  target/payment-rest-1.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar
```

## 15.2 Summary

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  -summary \
  target/payment-rest-1.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar
```

## 15.3 Print Module Dependencies

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  --print-module-deps \
  target/payment-bootstrap-1.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar
```

`jdeps` shows package-level or class-level dependencies, and options control the output. ([Oracle Documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/specs/man/jdeps.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 15.4 Generate DOT Graph

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  --dot-output target/jdeps-dot \
  target/payment-bootstrap-1.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar
```

Then:

```bash
dot -Tpng target/jdeps-dot/summary.dot -o target/jdeps-summary.png
```

## 15.5 Find JDK Internal API Usage

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  --jdk-internals \
  target/payment-bootstrap-1.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar
```

Oracle’s migration guidance recommends running `jdeps` to see packages/classes an application or libraries depend on, especially internal APIs. ([Oracle Documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/22/migrate/preparing-migration.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. Layer Violation Analysis

## 16.1 Rules

```text
payment-domain:
  must not depend on jakarta.ws.rs, jersey, mybatis, postgresql, cloud SDK

payment-application:
  must not depend on jakarta.ws.rs, jersey, mybatis, postgresql

payment-rest:
  must not depend on payment-persistence-mybatis, org.postgresql

payment-api:
  must not depend on payment-persistence-mybatis, org.mybatis, org.postgresql

payment-persistence-mybatis:
  must not depend on payment-rest, jakarta.ws.rs

payment-bootstrap:
  may depend on concrete adapters
```

## 16.2 Simple CI Script with Maven Tree

Generate JSON:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:tree \
  -DoutputType=json \
  -DoutputFile=payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json
```

Then simple grep guardrail:

```bash
if grep -q "payment-persistence-mybatis" payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json; then
  echo "ERROR: payment-rest must not depend on payment-persistence-mybatis"
  exit 1
fi
```

Better long-term: parse JSON with a script and produce readable violation output.

## 16.3 Example Python Guardrail

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

RULES = {
    "payment-domain/target/dependency-tree.json": [
        "jakarta.ws.rs",
        "org.glassfish.jersey",
        "org.mybatis",
        "org.postgresql",
    ],
    "payment-application/target/dependency-tree.json": [
        "jakarta.ws.rs",
        "org.glassfish.jersey",
        "org.mybatis",
        "org.postgresql",
    ],
    "payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json": [
        "payment-persistence-mybatis",
        "org.postgresql",
    ],
}

def walk(node):
    yield node
    for child in node.get("children", []) or []:
        yield from walk(child)

def gav(node):
    parts = [
        node.get("groupId", ""),
        node.get("artifactId", ""),
        node.get("version", ""),
    ]
    return ":".join(parts)

failed = False

for file_name, banned_fragments in RULES.items():
    path = Path(file_name)
    if not path.exists():
        print(f"SKIP: {path} not found")
        continue

    root = json.loads(path.read_text())
    artifacts = [gav(n) for n in walk(root)]

    for banned in banned_fragments:
        matches = [a for a in artifacts if banned in a]
        if matches:
            failed = True
            print(f"ERROR: {file_name} contains banned dependency fragment: {banned}")
            for match in matches:
                print(f"  - {match}")

if failed:
    sys.exit(1)

print("Dependency layer guardrail passed.")
```

---

# 17. `module-info.java` Consistency Audit

## 17.1 Maven POM Says Artifact Is Available

```xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>com.company.payment</groupId>
    <artifactId>payment-domain</artifactId>
    <version>${project.version}</version>
</dependency>
```

## 17.2 JPMS Says Module Is Readable

```java
module com.company.payment.application {
    requires com.company.payment.domain;
}
```

Both need to be aligned.

Audit questions:

| Check | Question |
|---|---|
| POM dependency exists, no `requires` | Is code compiled in classpath mode or missing JPMS readability? |
| `requires` exists, no POM dependency | Is dependency inherited/transitive accidentally? |
| `exports` package not used | Is it overexposed? |
| `opens` package not needed | Is reflection access too broad? |
| `requires transitive` used | Does public API really expose that dependency’s type? |
| `requires static` used | Is dependency truly optional at runtime? |

---

# 18. Runtime Packaging Audit

## 18.1 Classpath Fat JAR

```bash
java -jar payment-service.jar
```

Audit:

```text
1. Is this actually running in unnamed module/classpath mode?
2. Are duplicate classes hidden inside fat JAR?
3. Are service provider files merged correctly?
4. Are module-info.class files ignored or preserved meaningfully?
```

## 18.2 Thin JAR + Lib Folder

```bash
java -cp "app.jar:lib/*" com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Audit:

```text
1. Is lib folder complete?
2. Are dependency versions deterministic?
3. Is classpath ordering stable?
4. Does Docker copy the same lib set CI tested?
```

## 18.3 Module Path

```bash
java \
  --module-path "mods:lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Audit:

```text
1. Are all module names valid?
2. Are automatic modules acceptable?
3. Are split packages resolved?
4. Are reflective packages opened?
5. Does integration test run with this exact command style?
```

---

# 19. Docker/Kubernetes Parity

A common failure:

```text
Local test:
mvn test

CI build:
mvn verify

Docker:
java -jar app.jar

JPMS experiment:
java --module-path mods:lib --module ...
```

These are not equivalent.

## 19.1 Recommended CI Stages

```yaml
name: Build

on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  verify:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: '17'
          cache: maven

      - name: Maven verify
        run: mvn -B clean verify

      - name: Dependency tree reports
        run: |
          mvn -B -pl payment-rest dependency:tree \
            -DoutputType=json \
            -DoutputFile=payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json
          mvn -B -pl payment-application dependency:tree \
            -DoutputType=json \
            -DoutputFile=payment-application/target/dependency-tree.json
          mvn -B -pl payment-domain dependency:tree \
            -DoutputType=json \
            -DoutputFile=payment-domain/target/dependency-tree.json

      - name: Dependency layer guardrail
        run: python3 scripts/check-dependency-boundaries.py

      - name: jdeps report
        run: |
          jdeps --multi-release 17 \
            --summary \
            payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap-*.jar || true
```

Use `|| true` only while introducing the report. Once stable, make it fail the build where appropriate.

---

# 20. Maven Enforcer Baseline

## 20.1 Parent POM Example

```xml
<properties>
    <maven.enforcer.plugin.version>3.6.1</maven.enforcer.plugin.version>
    <extra.enforcer.rules.version>1.10.0</extra.enforcer.rules.version>
</properties>

<build>
    <pluginManagement>
        <plugins>
            <plugin>
                <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>
                <artifactId>maven-enforcer-plugin</artifactId>
                <version>${maven.enforcer.plugin.version}</version>
                <dependencies>
                    <dependency>
                        <groupId>org.codehaus.mojo</groupId>
                        <artifactId>extra-enforcer-rules</artifactId>
                        <version>${extra.enforcer.rules.version}</version>
                    </dependency>
                </dependencies>
            </plugin>
        </plugins>
    </pluginManagement>
</build>
```

## 20.2 Service Build Execution

```xml
<build>
    <plugins>
        <plugin>
            <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>
            <artifactId>maven-enforcer-plugin</artifactId>
            <executions>
                <execution>
                    <id>dependency-hygiene</id>
                    <phase>validate</phase>
                    <goals>
                        <goal>enforce</goal>
                    </goals>
                    <configuration>
                        <rules>
                            <dependencyConvergence/>

                            <requireUpperBoundDeps/>

                            <bannedDependencies>
                                <excludes>
                                    <exclude>javax.ws.rs:javax.ws.rs-api</exclude>
                                </excludes>
                                <searchTransitive>true</searchTransitive>
                            </bannedDependencies>

                            <banDuplicateClasses>
                                <findAllDuplicates>true</findAllDuplicates>
                            </banDuplicateClasses>
                        </rules>
                    </configuration>
                </execution>
            </executions>
        </plugin>
    </plugins>
</build>
```

Start strictness gradually. In a legacy enterprise codebase, enabling all rules at once can produce too much noise. Start with reporting, then fail only on high-confidence violations.

---

# 21. Practical Investigation Workflow

When a dependency issue appears, use this sequence:

```text
1. Identify symptom.
2. Locate module where symptom appears.
3. Run dependency:tree for that module.
4. Check direct vs transitive source.
5. Check dependencyManagement/BOM alignment.
6. Check duplicate classes.
7. Check module-info.java requires/exports/opens.
8. Check jdeps output.
9. Check runtime packaging command.
10. Add CI guardrail to prevent recurrence.
```

Example symptom:

```text
java.lang.NoSuchMethodError: com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper...
```

Investigation:

```bash
mvn dependency:tree -Dincludes=com.fasterxml.jackson.core
mvn dependency:tree -Dincludes=com.fasterxml.jackson.module
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:tree
```

Likely causes:

```text
1. Conflicting Jackson versions.
2. BOM not applied consistently.
3. Transitive dependency pulled older module.
4. Fat JAR packaged unexpected version.
```

Fix:

```text
1. Align Jackson versions using BOM/dependencyManagement.
2. Exclude old transitive dependency if needed.
3. Add dependency convergence rule.
4. Add test covering serialization path.
```

---

# 22. JPMS-Specific Failure Modes

| Symptom | Likely cause | Tool | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `module not found` | Wrong module name or missing JAR on module path | `jar --describe-module` | Fix module path/POM |
| `package is not visible` | Missing `exports` or wrong `requires` | compiler error, `jdeps` | Add correct `exports`/`requires` if design allows |
| `does not open package` | Reflection access missing | runtime error | Add minimal `opens` |
| Works classpath, fails module path | Split package, automatic module, missing opens | `jdeps`, module startup | Clean graph |
| `reads package from both` | Split package | startup/compiler error | Rename package or consolidate owner |
| Wrong service provider loaded | Fat JAR service files not merged | packaging inspection | Configure shade/assembly properly |
| Domain sees Jersey | Bad Maven dependency | `dependency:tree` | Remove dependency, fix layer |

---

# 23. Dependency Ownership Matrix

| Dependency | Owner module | Should not appear in |
|---|---|---|
| `jakarta.ws.rs-api` | `payment-rest` | `payment-domain`, `payment-application`, `payment-persistence` |
| Jersey server/runtime | `payment-bootstrap` or `payment-rest` depending layout | `payment-domain`, `payment-application` |
| `jakarta.validation-api` | `payment-api` or `payment-rest` if DTO validation | `payment-domain` unless intentionally domain validation annotation |
| JSON-B/Jackson provider | `payment-bootstrap`/REST runtime | `payment-domain`, `payment-persistence` unless adapter-specific |
| MyBatis | `payment-persistence-mybatis` | `payment-rest`, `payment-application`, `payment-domain` |
| PostgreSQL JDBC driver | `payment-persistence-mybatis` or `payment-bootstrap` runtime | `payment-rest`, `payment-domain`, `payment-api` |
| AWS/Azure SDK | specific integration/storage adapter | `domain`, generic `api` |
| Camunda client/engine | workflow adapter/application workflow module | generic domain unless workflow is core domain |
| Testcontainers | test scope only | runtime scope |
| JUnit/Mockito | test scope only | runtime scope |

---

# 24. Practical POM Hygiene

## 24.1 Avoid Inherited Dependencies in Parent

Bad parent POM:

```xml
<dependencies>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.glassfish.jersey.core</groupId>
        <artifactId>jersey-server</artifactId>
        <version>${jersey.version}</version>
    </dependency>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.mybatis</groupId>
        <artifactId>mybatis</artifactId>
        <version>${mybatis.version}</version>
    </dependency>
</dependencies>
```

This makes every child module inherit framework dependencies.

Better:

```xml
<dependencyManagement>
    <dependencies>
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.glassfish.jersey.core</groupId>
            <artifactId>jersey-server</artifactId>
            <version>${jersey.version}</version>
        </dependency>
        <dependency>
            <groupId>org.mybatis</groupId>
            <artifactId>mybatis</artifactId>
            <version>${mybatis.version}</version>
        </dependency>
    </dependencies>
</dependencyManagement>
```

Then each child declares only what it uses.

## 24.2 Scope Discipline

| Scope | Practical meaning |
|---|---|
| `compile` | Needed to compile and usually runtime |
| `runtime` | Not needed at compile, needed at runtime |
| `test` | Tests only |
| `provided` | Provided by runtime/container |
| `optional` | Not pulled transitively by consumers |

Example:

```xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.postgresql</groupId>
    <artifactId>postgresql</artifactId>
    <scope>runtime</scope>
</dependency>
```

PostgreSQL driver often does not need to be compiled against directly if the code uses JDBC interfaces. But if code imports driver-specific classes, compile scope may be needed. Prefer not to import driver-specific classes unless justified.

# 25. Lab 1 - Dependency Tree Baseline

Run:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-domain dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-api dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-application dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-persistence-mybatis dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:tree
```

Expected findings:

```text
payment-domain:
  minimal dependencies

payment-application:
  depends on payment-domain

payment-rest:
  depends on payment-api, payment-application, jakarta.ws.rs

payment-persistence-mybatis:
  depends on payment-domain, MyBatis, PostgreSQL runtime

payment-bootstrap:
  depends on rest/application/persistence/integration/runtime
```

# 26. Lab 2 - Catch REST → Persistence Leak

Intentionally add to `payment-rest/pom.xml`:

```xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>com.company.payment</groupId>
    <artifactId>payment-persistence-mybatis</artifactId>
    <version>${project.version}</version>
</dependency>
```

Run:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:tree
```

Expected:

```text
payment-persistence-mybatis appears.
```

Then implement guardrail:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:tree \
  -DoutputType=json \
  -DoutputFile=payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json

grep -q "payment-persistence-mybatis" payment-rest/target/dependency-tree.json \
  && echo "ERROR: REST depends on persistence" \
  && exit 1
```

Remove dependency and verify guardrail passes.

# 27. Lab 3 - Check Automatic Modules

Copy dependencies:

```bash
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:copy-dependencies \
  -DincludeScope=runtime \
  -DoutputDirectory=payment-bootstrap/target/dependency
```

Inspect:

```bash
for j in payment-bootstrap/target/dependency/*.jar; do
  echo "==== $j"
  jar --describe-module --file "$j" 2>/dev/null | head -30
done
```

Record:

```text
1. Explicit modules
2. Automatic modules
3. Unnamed/classpath-only dependencies
4. Module names used in module-info.java
```

Output artifact for team:

```text
docs/jpms-module-name-inventory.md
```

# 28. Lab 4 - `jdeps` Report

Build:

```bash
mvn clean package
```

Run:

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  --summary \
  payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap-*.jar
```

Then:

```bash
jdeps \
  --multi-release 17 \
  --dot-output target/jdeps \
  payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap-*.jar
```

Expected output:

```text
Readable dependency summary.
DOT files for dependency graph.
```

Ask during review:

```text
Does payment-domain show unexpected dependencies?
Does application show REST/Jersey?
Does REST show persistence?
```

---

# 29. Lab 5 - Enforcer Rollout

Stage 1: Report only.

```bash
mvn validate -Denforcer.skip=true
mvn dependency:tree
```

Stage 2: Enable banned dependency for obvious legacy dependency.

```xml
<bannedDependencies>
    <excludes>
        <exclude>javax.ws.rs:javax.ws.rs-api</exclude>
    </excludes>
    <searchTransitive>true</searchTransitive>
</bannedDependencies>
```

Stage 3: Enable convergence.

```xml
<dependencyConvergence/>
```

Stage 4: Enable upper bound.

```xml
<requireUpperBoundDeps/>
```

Stage 5: Enable duplicate classes after initial cleanup.

```xml
<banDuplicateClasses>
    <findAllDuplicates>true</findAllDuplicates>
</banDuplicateClasses>
```

Practical advice:

```text
Do not turn every rule into hard fail on day one.
Start with high-confidence rules, publish reports, then tighten.
```

---

# 30. CI Guardrail Template

```yaml
name: Dependency Hygiene

on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  dependency-hygiene:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: '17'
          cache: maven

      - name: Build and test
        run: mvn -B clean verify

      - name: Generate dependency trees
        run: |
          for module in payment-domain payment-api payment-application payment-rest payment-persistence-mybatis payment-bootstrap; do
            mvn -B -pl "$module" dependency:tree \
              -DoutputType=json \
              -DoutputFile="$module/target/dependency-tree.json"
          done

      - name: Check dependency boundaries
        run: python3 scripts/check-dependency-boundaries.py

      - name: Generate jdeps summary
        run: |
          mkdir -p target/jdeps
          jdeps --multi-release 17 \
            --summary \
            payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap-*.jar \
            > target/jdeps/summary.txt || true

      - name: Upload dependency reports
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: dependency-hygiene-reports
          path: |
            **/target/dependency-tree.json
            target/jdeps/summary.txt
```

---

# 31. PR Review Checklist

| Area | Question |
|---|---|
| New dependency | Which module owns this dependency? |
| Scope | Is the scope correct: compile/runtime/test/provided? |
| Transitive impact | What new transitive dependencies appear? |
| Layering | Does this create REST → persistence or domain → framework dependency? |
| Version alignment | Does it conflict with existing BOM/dependencyManagement? |
| JPMS | Does module-info.java need `requires`, `exports`, or `opens`? |
| Reflection | Is `opens` minimal and justified? |
| Runtime packaging | Will Docker/fat JAR include the correct dependency? |
| Duplicate classes | Does it introduce duplicate classes or shaded overlap? |
| CVE/compliance | Does dependency introduce known risk or banned license/security issue? |

---

# 32. Common Mistakes

| Mistake | Impact | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| Putting all dependencies in parent `<dependencies>` | Every module sees every framework | Use `dependencyManagement`; declare per child |
| Ignoring transitive dependencies | Hidden version/runtime conflicts | Inspect `dependency:tree` |
| Adding exclusions blindly | Can break runtime behavior | Understand dependency path first |
| Treating `dependencyManagement` as dependency | Build confusion | Child still needs `<dependency>` |
| Letting REST depend on persistence | Business rule bypass | Wire persistence in bootstrap |
| Guessing module names | JPMS build/runtime error | Use `jar --describe-module` |
| Ignoring duplicate classes | Class shadowing | Use `banDuplicateClasses` |
| Running only classpath tests | Module-path issues hidden | Add module-path smoke test if target runtime is modular |
| Leaving temporary `--add-opens` forever | Hidden architecture debt | Move valid opens to `module-info.java` or remove |

---

# 33. Practical Recommendation

For a Jersey/JAX-RS microservice using Maven + JPMS:

```text
1. Make dependency tree review part of every PR touching pom.xml.
2. Keep parent POM for versions, not inherited framework dependencies.
3. Make domain dependency tree intentionally boring.
4. Block REST → persistence dependency.
5. Block application → Jersey/MyBatis/PostgreSQL dependency.
6. Use dependency convergence and upper bound checks after initial cleanup.
7. Detect duplicate classes before production packaging.
8. Inventory actual module names before module-path runtime.
9. Treat jdeps output as architecture evidence.
10. Align local, CI, Docker, and Kubernetes startup mode.
```

The real target is not “zero dependencies”. The target is:

```text
Every dependency has an owner.
Every owner has a reason.
Every transitive dependency is visible.
Every module boundary can be defended.
```

**Seri 7/12 selesai - Dependency Hygiene & Module Graph Analysis.**

**Sources:**

- [dependency:tree - Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/plugins/maven-dependency-plugin/tree-mojo.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [The jdeps Command](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/specs/man/jdeps.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- [Ban Duplicate Classes – Extra Enforcer Rules](https://www.mojohaus.org/extra-enforcer-rules/banDuplicateClasses.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Response:
7/15/2026, 1:26:59 AM

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 8/12**. Fokusnya strategi migrasi aman: kapan tetap classpath, kapan mulai module path, bagaimana menjalankan dual-mode test, dan cara menghindari big-bang migration.

# Seri 8/12 - Classpath vs Module Path Migration Strategy

## 1. Introduction

Di seri ini kita bahas strategi transisi dari aplikasi Java/Jersey/JAX-RS yang masih berjalan dengan **classpath / fat JAR** menuju aplikasi yang **JPMS-aware** dan, jika memang layak, bisa berjalan dengan **module path**.

Poin pentingnya:

```text id="92lmc4"
Classpath migration ≠ langsung full module-path runtime.
```

Untuk aplikasi enterprise berbasis **Jersey/JAX-RS, MyBatis, PostgreSQL, JSON serializer, validator, Docker, Kubernetes**, pendekatan paling aman adalah **bertahap**:

```text id="5fx8qs"
1. Rapikan Maven dependency.
2. Rapikan boundary module internal.
3. Tambahkan module-info.java ke module yang minim reflection.
4. Jalankan tetap di classpath dulu jika runtime belum siap.
5. Audit dependency/module name/split package.
6. Baru uji module path sebagai mode eksperimen.
7. Jika stabil, jadikan module path runtime resmi.
```

JPMS sendiri memperkenalkan module path sebagai lokasi untuk compiled module definitions, dan module definitions di module path bersama system modules membentuk universe of observable modules. Ini berbeda dari classpath yang historisnya lebih longgar. ([OpenJDK](https://openjdk.org/jeps/261?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Mental Model: Classpath vs Module Path

## 2.1 Classpath Mode

Classpath mode:

```bash id="0bin88"
java -cp "app.jar:lib/*" com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

atau fat JAR:

```bash id="vno3de"
java -jar payment-service.jar
```

Karakteristik:

```text id="u7lzaq"
1. Compatibility tinggi.
2. Semua JAR/class di classpath masuk unnamed module.
3. Boundary JPMS runtime tidak benar-benar strict.
4. Reflection-heavy framework biasanya lebih mudah jalan.
5. Banyak aplikasi enterprise masih memakai model ini.
```

Dev.java menjelaskan bahwa semua JAR di classpath, modular atau tidak, menjadi bagian dari **unnamed module**; bahkan modular JAR yang diletakkan di classpath tidak otomatis dimuat sebagai named module. ([Dev.java](https://dev.java/learn/code-on-the-class-path---the-unnamed-module/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Implikasi praktis:

```text id="23to2t"
Kalau service masih jalan dengan java -jar atau -cp,
module-info.java tetap berguna untuk compile-time structure,
tetapi runtime tidak sama strict-nya dengan module path.
```

---

## 2.2 Module Path Mode

Module path mode:

```bash id="29ltc8"
java \
  --module-path "mods:lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Karakteristik:

```text id="dcz9cb"
1. Module graph eksplisit.
2. requires harus benar.
3. exports menentukan package yang bisa diakses normal.
4. opens menentukan package yang bisa diakses reflection.
5. Split package lebih cepat ketahuan.
6. Automatic module bisa menjadi risiko.
7. Framework reflection perlu dikontrol.
```

Module path bukan “classpath versi baru”. Module path mencari **module definitions**, bukan sekadar semua class yang tersedia.

---

# 3. Diagram Strategi Migrasi

```text id="f14gqt"
Existing Enterprise Jersey Service
│
├── Stage 0 - Baseline
│   ├── java -jar / classpath runtime
│   ├── Maven dependency tree
│   ├── no module-info.java or partial module-info.java
│   └── framework works as-is
│
├── Stage 1 - Dependency Hygiene
│   ├── dependency:tree
│   ├── dependency convergence
│   ├── ban legacy dependency
│   ├── detect duplicate classes
│   └── identify REST → persistence leak
│
├── Stage 2 - Internal Modularization
│   ├── payment-domain module-info.java
│   ├── payment-application module-info.java
│   ├── payment-api module-info.java
│   └── compile-time boundary enforcement
│
├── Stage 3 - Reflection Mapping
│   ├── DTO opens
│   ├── REST resource/provider opens
│   ├── MyBatis mapper/entity opens
│   └── document every opens
│
├── Stage 4 - Dual Runtime Testing
│   ├── classpath smoke test
│   ├── module-path experiment
│   ├── jdeps report
│   ├── jar --describe-module inventory
│   └── Docker parity check
│
├── Stage 5 - Module Path Hardening
│   ├── fix split package
│   ├── reduce open module
│   ├── reduce unqualified opens
│   ├── replace --add-opens with module-info.java
│   └── explicit Jersey registration
│
└── Stage 6 - Production Decision
    ├── stay classpath but JPMS-aware
    ├── use module path runtime
    ├── use jlink runtime image
    └── document operational standard
```

---

# 4. Decomposition Map

| Area | Skill | Practical implementation | Risk if ignored |
|---|---|---|---|
| Runtime mode identification | Tahu app jalan via classpath atau module path | Cek Dockerfile, Helm chart, startup script | Salah klaim “sudah modular” padahal runtime classpath |
| Baseline dependency audit | Pahami dependency sebelum migrasi | `mvn dependency:tree`, `jdeps`, `jar --describe-module` | Migrasi gagal karena hidden transitive dependency |
| Module prioritization | Pilih module yang aman dimodularisasi dulu | Mulai dari domain/application/API | Big-bang migration terlalu mahal |
| Reflection inventory | Catat package yang butuh reflection | DTO, resource, provider, mapper, entity | Runtime `IllegalAccessException` |
| Classpath compatibility | Tetap menjaga existing deployment stabil | Run `java -jar` smoke test | Regression production |
| Module-path experiment | Uji strict runtime secara terpisah | Run `java --module-path ... --module ...` | Masalah module path tidak terdeteksi |
| Automatic module inventory | Tahu nama module dependency non-modular | `jar --describe-module` | Salah `requires` / `opens to` |
| Split package cleanup | Pastikan satu package dimiliki satu module | Rename package atau extract owner module | Module path gagal |
| Temporary flags governance | Kelola `--add-opens`, `--add-exports` | Document owner, reason, expiry | Technical debt permanen |
| CI dual-mode | Test classpath + optional module path | GitHub Actions stage | Local/CI/Docker tidak konsisten |
| Docker parity | Startup command di container sama dengan test | Validate Docker entrypoint | Works in CI, fails in Kubernetes |
| Production decision | Pilih runtime mode realistis | classpath JPMS-aware vs full module path | Over-engineering atau false security |

---

# 5. Stage 0 - Baseline Existing Runtime

Sebelum bicara migrasi, jawab dulu:

```text id="kbqkio"
Service sekarang jalan dengan apa?
```

## 5.1 Cek Dockerfile

Classpath/fat JAR style:

```dockerfile id="bfjvzr"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app
COPY target/payment-service.jar app.jar
ENTRYPOINT ["java", "-jar", "app.jar"]
```

Thin JAR + lib folder:

```dockerfile id="rypurb"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app
COPY target/payment-bootstrap.jar app.jar
COPY target/lib lib
ENTRYPOINT ["java", "-cp", "app.jar:lib/*", "com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main"]
```

Module path style:

```dockerfile id="qr1g6z"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app
COPY mods mods
COPY lib lib
ENTRYPOINT [
  "java",
  "--module-path", "mods:lib",
  "--module", "com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main"
]
```

## 5.2 Practical conclusion

Jika masih:

```bash id="dbaf8k"
java -jar app.jar
```

maka service kemungkinan masih classpath-style runtime.

Artinya:

```text id="mlqsl2"
module-info.java bisa tetap berguna untuk compile/build boundary,
tetapi jangan menganggap runtime sudah strict JPMS.
```

---

# 6. Stage 1 - Dependency Hygiene Sebelum JPMS

Jangan mulai dengan `module-info.java` jika dependency graph masih kacau.

## 6.1 Commands baseline

```bash id="h0uj5q"
mvn clean verify
mvn dependency:tree
```

Per module:

```bash id="5p4dd6"
mvn -pl payment-domain dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-api dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-application dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-rest dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-persistence-mybatis dependency:tree
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:tree
```

## 6.2 Expected healthy graph

```text id="5t0h71"
payment-domain
└── minimal dependency

payment-api
└── DTO / validation dependencies only if needed

payment-application
└── payment-domain

payment-rest
├── payment-api
├── payment-application
└── jakarta.ws.rs / Jersey-related dependency

payment-persistence-mybatis
├── payment-domain
├── MyBatis
└── PostgreSQL runtime

payment-bootstrap
├── payment-rest
├── payment-application
├── payment-persistence-mybatis
├── integration adapters
└── runtime/server dependencies
```

## 6.3 Red flags

```text id="umhfoj"
payment-domain → jakarta.ws.rs
payment-domain → org.mybatis
payment-application → org.postgresql
payment-rest → payment-persistence-mybatis
payment-api → payment-persistence-mybatis
payment-api → org.mybatis
```

Fix dulu ini sebelum module-path migration.

---

# 7. Stage 2 - Modularisasi Internal yang Minim Reflection

Urutan aman:

```text id="vj2b19"
1. payment-domain
2. payment-application
3. payment-api
4. payment-integration-spi
5. payment-rest
6. payment-persistence-mybatis
7. payment-bootstrap
```

## 7.1 Kenapa mulai dari domain?

Karena domain idealnya:

```text id="zwiobe"
1. Tidak butuh Jersey.
2. Tidak butuh MyBatis.
3. Tidak butuh PostgreSQL.
4. Tidak butuh serializer.
5. Minim reflection.
6. Boundary-nya paling mudah ditegakkan.
```

`payment-domain/module-info.java`:

```java id="amfjhw"
module com.company.payment.domain {
    exports com.company.payment.domain.model;
    exports com.company.payment.domain.valueobject;
    exports com.company.payment.domain.port;
    exports com.company.payment.domain.event;
    exports com.company.payment.domain.exception;
}
```

## 7.2 Application module

```java id="bse8wv"
module com.company.payment.application {
    requires com.company.payment.domain;

    exports com.company.payment.application.command;
    exports com.company.payment.application.query;
    exports com.company.payment.application.usecase;
}
```

## 7.3 API module

```java id="fg4dxq"
module com.company.payment.api {
    exports com.company.payment.api.request;
    exports com.company.payment.api.response;
    exports com.company.payment.api.error;

    opens com.company.payment.api.request;
    opens com.company.payment.api.response;
    opens com.company.payment.api.error;
}
```

API DTO mulai perlu `opens` karena serializer/validator sering memakai reflection.

---

# 8. Stage 3 - Compile-time JPMS, Runtime Tetap Classpath

Ini strategi yang paling realistis untuk banyak service enterprise.

## 8.1 Target stage ini

```text id="mh0ynd"
Compile/build mulai JPMS-aware.
Runtime tetap classpath agar existing deployment stabil.
```

Artinya:

```text id="apec7o"
- module-info.java dipakai untuk menjaga boundary source/build.
- mvn clean verify harus lulus.
- java -jar / classpath runtime tetap jalan.
- module path belum wajib untuk production.
```

## 8.2 Benefit langsung

Walau runtime masih classpath, module descriptor tetap memberi manfaat:

```text id="aos1kb"
1. Developer melihat dependency direction eksplisit.
2. Package internal tidak sembarang diexport saat compile modular.
3. Dependency bocor lebih mudah terlihat.
4. IDE/build bisa mulai memahami module boundary.
5. Tim bisa menyiapkan module path tanpa mengganggu deployment.
```

## 8.3 Caveat penting

Karena runtime masih classpath/unnamed module, enforcement runtime tidak setara module path.

Dev.java menyatakan bahwa classpath dikelola unnamed module, dan modular JAR di classpath tidak otomatis dimuat sebagai module. Jadi boundary yang Anda rasakan di compile/build bisa lebih ketat daripada runtime classpath. ([Dev.java](https://dev.java/learn/code-on-the-class-path---the-unnamed-module/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 9. Stage 4 - Module Path Experiment

Setelah internal module relatif bersih, buat eksperimen module path.

## 9.1 Build modular artifacts

```bash id="jnyy8v"
mvn clean package
```

Siapkan dependency runtime:

```bash id="xy7x1k"
mvn -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:copy-dependencies \
  -DincludeScope=runtime \
  -DoutputDirectory=payment-bootstrap/target/lib
```

Struktur target:

```text id="jodmwn"
runtime/
├── mods/
│   ├── payment-domain.jar
│   ├── payment-api.jar
│   ├── payment-application.jar
│   ├── payment-rest.jar
│   ├── payment-persistence-mybatis.jar
│   └── payment-bootstrap.jar
└── lib/
    ├── jersey-server.jar
    ├── mybatis.jar
    ├── postgresql.jar
    └── other dependencies
```

## 9.2 Inventory module names

```bash id="khxrek"
for j in runtime/mods/*.jar runtime/lib/*.jar; do
  echo "==== $j"
  jar --describe-module --file "$j" 2>/dev/null | head -30
done
```

Look for:

```text id="rsycid"
1. explicit module
2. automatic module
3. derived automatic module name
4. missing module descriptor
5. unexpected module name
```

## 9.3 Run module path

```bash id="o6f33j"
java \
  --module-path "runtime/mods:runtime/lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Expected first run:

```text id="2y3z3d"
Tidak selalu langsung berhasil.
Module path experiment memang untuk menemukan masalah.
```

Typical failure:

```text id="5io00t"
module not found
package is not visible
does not export package
does not open package
reads package from both modules
service provider not found
```

---

# 10. Stage 5 - Reflection Failure Handling

## 10.1 Jangan langsung pakai `open module`

Jika error:

```text id="aasyei"
module com.company.payment.api does not "opens com.company.payment.api.request"
to module com.fasterxml.jackson.databind
```

Jangan langsung:

```java id="2n4dit"
open module com.company.payment.api {
}
```

Lebih baik:

```java id="i7q7u2"
module com.company.payment.api {
    exports com.company.payment.api.request;
    exports com.company.payment.api.response;

    opens com.company.payment.api.request to com.fasterxml.jackson.databind;
    opens com.company.payment.api.response to com.fasterxml.jackson.databind;
}
```

Jika nama target module belum pasti:

```java id="tjtmzw"
opens com.company.payment.api.request;
```

lalu dokumentasikan sebagai sementara.

## 10.2 Temporary runtime flag

Untuk diagnosis:

```bash id="hn2ick"
java \
  --add-opens com.company.payment.api/com.company.payment.api.request=com.fasterxml.jackson.databind \
  --module-path "runtime/mods:runtime/lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Oracle migration guide menjelaskan opsi `--add-exports` untuk memberi akses ke package dari source module ke target module; JPMS juga menyediakan opsi terkait seperti `--add-opens` untuk kasus reflective access. Gunakan flag seperti ini sebagai bridge/diagnostic, bukan desain permanen. ([Oracle Documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/17/migrate/migrating-jdk-8-later-jdk-releases.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 10.3 Governance untuk temporary flags

Buat file:

```text id="n2608q"
docs/jpms-runtime-flags.md
```

Isi:

```text id="qv3vx9"
Flag:
--add-opens com.company.payment.api/com.company.payment.api.request=com.fasterxml.jackson.databind

Reason:
Jackson deserialization needs reflective access to request DTO.

Owner:
Payment Platform Team

Target fix:
Move to module-info.java qualified opens after module name inventory is stable.

Expiry:
Before module-path production rollout.
```

---

# 11. Stage 6 - Split Package Cleanup

Module path bisa gagal jika package sama dimiliki beberapa module.

## 11.1 Bad example

```text id="rvzv6w"
payment-domain.jar
└── com.company.payment.common.DateRange

payment-api.jar
└── com.company.payment.common.ErrorResponse

payment-rest.jar
└── com.company.payment.common.RestErrorMapper
```

## 11.2 Better ownership

```text id="2wx2qn"
payment-domain.jar
└── com.company.payment.domain.common.DateRange

payment-api.jar
└── com.company.payment.api.error.ErrorResponse

payment-rest.jar
└── com.company.payment.rest.mapper.RestErrorMapper
```

## 11.3 Detection script

```bash id="kl1wnk"
mkdir -p target/all-jars

find . -path "*/target/*.jar" -type f -exec cp {} target/all-jars/ \;

for jar in target/all-jars/*.jar; do
  jar tf "$jar" \
    | grep '\.class$' \
    | sed 's|/[^/]*\.class$||' \
    | sed 's|/|.|g' \
    | sort -u \
    | awk -v jar="$jar" '{ print $0 " " jar }'
done \
| sort \
| awk '
{
  pkg=$1
  jar=$2
  jars[pkg]=jars[pkg] " " jar
  count[pkg]++
}
END {
  for (pkg in count) {
    if (count[pkg] > 1) {
      print pkg ":" jars[pkg]
    }
  }
}'
```

Review manual tetap perlu, karena multi-release JAR dan beberapa library bisa menghasilkan noise.

---

# 12. Stage 7 - Jersey/JAX-RS Runtime Strategy

Untuk Jersey service, runtime migration harus memperhatikan resource/provider registration.

## 12.1 Prefer explicit registration saat migrasi

```java id="7fmyil"
public class PaymentApplicationConfig extends ResourceConfig {

    public PaymentApplicationConfig() {
        register(paymentResource());
        register(PaymentNotFoundExceptionMapper.class);
        register(CorrelationIdFilter.class);
    }

    private PaymentResource paymentResource() {
        var repository = new MyBatisPaymentRepository();
        var useCase = new CreatePaymentUseCase(repository);
        var mapper = new PaymentRestMapper();

        return new PaymentResource(useCase, mapper);
    }
}
```

Lebih mudah didiagnosis daripada:

```java id="8041xw"
packages("com.company.payment.rest");
```

## 12.2 Kenapa?

Dalam module path:

```text id="2k4oxg"
1. Discovery/scanning lebih sensitif terhadap package boundary.
2. Provider yang tidak terdaftar eksplisit bisa tidak aktif.
3. Constructor injection/resource instantiation bisa gagal lebih tersembunyi.
4. Reflection access harus jelas.
```

## 12.3 REST module descriptor

```java id="v5xayr"
module com.company.payment.rest {
    requires jakarta.ws.rs;
    requires com.company.payment.api;
    requires com.company.payment.application;
    requires com.company.payment.domain;

    exports com.company.payment.rest.resource
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    exports com.company.payment.rest.exception
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    exports com.company.payment.rest.filter
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    opens com.company.payment.rest.resource;
    opens com.company.payment.rest.exception;
    opens com.company.payment.rest.filter;
    opens com.company.payment.rest.provider;
}
```

---

# 13. Stage 8 - MyBatis Runtime Strategy

MyBatis module biasanya lebih tricky karena mapper, XML, result mapping, type handler, dan entity construction.

## 13.1 Recommended descriptor

```java id="6z5v2s"
module com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis {
    requires java.sql;
    requires com.company.payment.domain;

    // requires org.mybatis; // validate actual module name

    exports com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.repository
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.mapper;
    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.entity;
    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.typehandler;
}
```

## 13.2 Recommended XML style during migration

Gunakan fully qualified class name dulu:

```xml id="a8r4hz"
<mapper namespace="com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.mapper.PaymentMapper">

  <resultMap id="PaymentResultMap"
             type="com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.entity.PaymentRecord">
    <id property="id" column="id"/>
    <result property="amount" column="amount"/>
    <result property="currency" column="currency"/>
    <result property="status" column="status"/>
  </resultMap>

</mapper>
```

Setelah stabil, baru optimasi dengan type alias/package scanning jika dibutuhkan.

## 13.3 Test wajib

```text id="xloz9f"
1. Mapper XML loaded.
2. Mapper interface registered.
3. SELECT result mapping works.
4. INSERT/UPDATE parameter mapping works.
5. TypeHandler works.
6. Transaction/session lifecycle works.
7. Run test in same runtime mode intended for deployment.
```

---

# 14. Dual-Mode Testing Strategy

Selama migrasi, ada dua runtime yang perlu diuji:

```text id="qsnndd"
1. Existing classpath runtime
2. Experimental module-path runtime
```

## 14.1 Classpath smoke test

```bash id="0vaq8y"
java -jar payment-service.jar
```

or:

```bash id="5i5s70"
java -cp "payment-bootstrap.jar:lib/*" \
  com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Smoke test:

```bash id="o8yo1q"
curl -f http://localhost:8080/health
curl -f http://localhost:8080/payments/test-id
```

## 14.2 Module-path smoke test

```bash id="op65e4"
java \
  --module-path "mods:lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Smoke test same endpoints.

## 14.3 CI mode

At first:

```text id="ju0pa2"
Classpath smoke test = blocking
Module-path smoke test = non-blocking/report-only
```

Later:

```text id="e64zb5"
Classpath smoke test = blocking
Module-path smoke test = blocking if module-path is target runtime
```

---

# 15. GitHub Actions Example

```yaml id="w07064"
name: JPMS Migration Check

on:
  pull_request:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  jpms-migration:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: '17'
          cache: maven

      - name: Build
        run: mvn -B clean verify

      - name: Dependency tree
        run: |
          for module in payment-domain payment-api payment-application payment-rest payment-persistence-mybatis payment-bootstrap; do
            mvn -B -pl "$module" dependency:tree \
              -DoutputType=json \
              -DoutputFile="$module/target/dependency-tree.json"
          done

      - name: Copy runtime dependencies
        run: |
          mvn -B -pl payment-bootstrap dependency:copy-dependencies \
            -DincludeScope=runtime \
            -DoutputDirectory=payment-bootstrap/target/lib

      - name: Inspect module names
        run: |
          mkdir -p target/jpms
          for j in payment-bootstrap/target/lib/*.jar; do
            echo "==== $j" >> target/jpms/module-name-inventory.txt
            jar --describe-module --file "$j" >> target/jpms/module-name-inventory.txt 2>&1 || true
          done

      - name: jdeps report
        run: |
          mkdir -p target/jpms
          jdeps --multi-release 17 \
            --summary \
            payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap-*.jar \
            > target/jpms/jdeps-summary.txt || true

      - name: Upload JPMS reports
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: jpms-migration-reports
          path: |
            target/jpms/*.txt
            **/target/dependency-tree.json
```

Tahap awal `jar --describe-module` dan `jdeps` boleh report-only. Setelah stabil, rule tertentu bisa dibuat blocking.

`jdeps` memang ditujukan untuk menganalisis dependency package/class dari class file, directory, atau JAR, sehingga cocok sebagai audit tool selama migrasi. ([Oracle Documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/17/migrate/migrating-jdk-8-later-jdk-releases.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. Migration Decision Matrix

| Kondisi service | Rekomendasi |
|---|---|
| Banyak reflection-heavy dependency | Tetap classpath runtime dulu, JPMS untuk internal boundary |
| Domain/application sudah bersih | Tambahkan `module-info.java` |
| REST masih package scanning luas | Prefer explicit registration dulu |
| MyBatis mapper/entity belum jelas | Jangan module-path production dulu |
| Banyak automatic modules | Inventory dan test dulu |
| Banyak split package | Cleanup sebelum module path |
| Docker masih `java -jar` | Jangan klaim runtime modular |
| CI belum punya smoke test | Jangan ubah runtime mode |
| Semua module path tests stabil | Pertimbangkan module path runtime |
| Ingin image lebih kecil/locked runtime | Evaluasi `jlink` setelah module graph bersih |

---

# 17. Migration Patterns

## Pattern A - JPMS-aware compile, classpath runtime

```text id="bs9y1e"
Use when:
- enterprise service existing
- Jersey/MyBatis/reflection-heavy
- deployment already stable with java -jar
- team wants boundary improvement without runtime risk
```

Implementation:

```text id="2bvowj"
1. Add module-info.java to internal modules.
2. Keep Docker entrypoint as java -jar.
3. Add dependency guardrail.
4. Add jdeps/module-name report.
5. Do not force module path yet.
```

Good for most teams as first target.

---

## Pattern B - Hybrid migration

```text id="js50d4"
Use when:
- some modules are explicit JPMS modules
- third-party dependencies include automatic modules
- classpath still exists for compatibility
```

Implementation:

```text id="j94y73"
1. Internal modules explicit.
2. Third-party dependencies audited.
3. Some automatic modules accepted temporarily.
4. Runtime module path tested but not necessarily production default.
```

Risk:

```text id="cxu9mm"
Automatic module names and behavior can be fragile.
Document inventory.
```

---

## Pattern C - Full module-path runtime

```text id="4ilczt"
Use when:
- split packages cleaned
- reflection opens documented
- third-party module names stable
- integration tests pass on module path
- Docker/Kubernetes entrypoint updated
```

Implementation:

```bash id="81lirf"
java \
  --module-path "mods:lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

This is stricter and cleaner, but not always worth the operational cost for every microservice.

---

## Pattern D - Custom runtime image with `jlink`

```text id="45fz58"
Use when:
- full module path is stable
- you want smaller runtime image
- operational team accepts custom runtime build
- CI/CD can reproduce runtime image
```

Do this after module path is stable, not before.

---

# 18. Practical Migration Plan: 6 Iterations

## Iteration 1 - Baseline evidence

Output:

```text id="gmv0dp"
docs/runtime-mode.md
docs/dependency-baseline.md
docs/module-name-inventory.md
```

Commands:

```bash id="cibvgk"
mvn clean verify
mvn dependency:tree
jdeps --multi-release 17 --summary target/*.jar
```

---

## Iteration 2 - Domain/application JPMS

Add:

```text id="93ind4"
payment-domain/src/main/java/module-info.java
payment-application/src/main/java/module-info.java
```

Success criteria:

```text id="dyzs0f"
1. Domain has no framework dependency.
2. Application only requires domain.
3. REST cannot accidentally import domain internals.
```

---

## Iteration 3 - API DTO JPMS

Add:

```java id="jzqlx0"
module com.company.payment.api {
    exports com.company.payment.api.request;
    exports com.company.payment.api.response;
    exports com.company.payment.api.error;

    opens com.company.payment.api.request;
    opens com.company.payment.api.response;
    opens com.company.payment.api.error;
}
```

Success criteria:

```text id="j65nyz"
1. JSON request deserialization works.
2. JSON response serialization works.
3. Validation still works if used.
```

---

## Iteration 4 - REST JPMS

Add:

```java id="a65alb"
module com.company.payment.rest {
    requires jakarta.ws.rs;
    requires com.company.payment.api;
    requires com.company.payment.application;
    requires com.company.payment.domain;

    exports com.company.payment.rest.resource
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    opens com.company.payment.rest.resource;
    opens com.company.payment.rest.exception;
    opens com.company.payment.rest.filter;
}
```

Success criteria:

```text id="m6z9iy"
1. Resource registered explicitly.
2. Exception mapper works.
3. Filter/provider works.
4. REST has no persistence dependency.
```

---

## Iteration 5 - Persistence JPMS

Add:

```java id="yyi4fj"
module com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis {
    requires java.sql;
    requires com.company.payment.domain;

    exports com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.repository
        to com.company.payment.bootstrap;

    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.mapper;
    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.entity;
    opens com.company.payment.persistence.mybatis.typehandler;
}
```

Success criteria:

```text id="tu4a54"
1. Mapper XML loads.
2. Result mapping works.
3. TypeHandler works.
4. Repository implementation not visible to REST.
```

---

## Iteration 6 - Runtime experiment

Create scripts:

```text id="ydvcjf"
scripts/run-classpath.sh
scripts/run-modulepath.sh
scripts/check-jpms-modules.sh
```

`run-classpath.sh`:

```bash id="owuiwv"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

java -cp "payment-bootstrap/target/payment-bootstrap.jar:payment-bootstrap/target/lib/*" \
  com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

`run-modulepath.sh`:

```bash id="cig2qf"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

java \
  --module-path "runtime/mods:runtime/lib" \
  --module com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main
```

Success criteria:

```text id="5udlw4"
1. Classpath runtime remains green.
2. Module path runtime failure list is known.
3. Every failure has owner.
4. Temporary --add-opens is documented.
```

---

# 19. Common Migration Failure Modes

| Failure | Example symptom | Root cause | Practical fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| Module not found | `Module org.mybatis not found` | Wrong module name / JAR not on module path | `jar --describe-module`, fix module path |
| Package not visible | `package ... is not visible` | Missing `exports` or `requires` | Add only if architecture allows |
| Reflection denied | `does not "opens"` | Serializer/validator/Jersey/MyBatis needs reflection | Add minimal `opens` |
| Split package | `reads package ... from both` | Same package in multiple modules | Rename/consolidate package |
| REST endpoint 404 | Resource not registered/discovered | Package scanning issue | Explicit `register(...)` |
| Exception mapper ignored | Provider not registered | Missing registration/scanning | Explicitly register mapper |
| MyBatis mapper not found | XML namespace/resource not loaded | Resource packaging/mapper config | Use FQCN, verify resources |
| Works local, fails Docker | Different startup command | Local classpath, Docker module path or vice versa | Align runtime scripts |
| Test passes, prod fails | Tests not run in target mode | CI lacks runtime smoke test | Add smoke test with actual entrypoint |
| Too many `--add-opens` | Migration patching symptoms | Reflection inventory not understood | Move valid opens to module descriptors |

---

# 20. Runtime Flag Policy

Temporary flags boleh dipakai untuk diagnosis:

```bash id="d9i2xe"
--add-opens
--add-exports
--add-reads
--add-modules
--patch-module
```

JEP 261 defines options like `--add-exports` and `--add-opens` as ways to adjust module access at compile/run time; these should be treated carefully because they can bypass intended encapsulation. ([OpenJDK](https://openjdk.org/jeps/261?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Maven Compiler Plugin 4.x documentation also discusses test-time needs such as `--patch-module`, `--add-modules`, `--add-reads`, `--add-exports`, and `--add-opens`, especially for white-box testing. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/plugins/maven-compiler-plugin-4.x/module-info-patch.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Policy praktis:

```text id="pbdbj8"
1. Runtime flag harus punya owner.
2. Runtime flag harus punya alasan.
3. Runtime flag harus punya expiry/removal plan.
4. Runtime flag tidak boleh ditambahkan diam-diam di Dockerfile.
5. Jika flag permanen, evaluasi apakah harus pindah ke module-info.java.
```

---

# 21. Maven Test Migration Note

Testing modular app sering butuh akses ekstra ke internal package. Jangan membuka production module hanya demi test.

Contoh buruk:

```java id="gq2til"
open module com.company.payment.domain {
    exports com.company.payment.domain.model;
}
```

Hanya karena unit test ingin akses internal class.

Lebih baik:

```text id="sn2j5m"
1. Test through public API where possible.
2. Use test-specific module patching where needed.
3. Keep production module descriptor strict.
```

Maven Compiler Plugin 4.x menyediakan konsep `module-info-patch.maven` untuk menyederhanakan white-box testing modular, daripada mengulang banyak compiler/runtime options manual. ([Apache Maven](https://maven.apache.org/plugins/maven-compiler-plugin-4.x/module-info-patch.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Detail testing akan dibahas khusus di **Seri 10/12**.

---

# 22. Docker Migration Strategy

## 22.1 Current classpath Docker

```dockerfile id="hlbti6"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app
COPY target/payment-service.jar app.jar
ENTRYPOINT ["java", "-jar", "app.jar"]
```

## 22.2 JPMS-aware classpath Docker

Tetap classpath, tetapi dengan reports/guardrail di CI:

```dockerfile id="7kx9wa"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app
COPY payment-bootstrap/target/payment-service.jar app.jar
ENTRYPOINT ["java", "-jar", "app.jar"]
```

Artinya:

```text id="l7mdvw"
Runtime belum module path,
tetapi source/build sudah mulai modular.
```

## 22.3 Module path Docker

```dockerfile id="jlsx5a"
FROM eclipse-temurin:17-jre
WORKDIR /app

COPY runtime/mods mods
COPY runtime/lib lib

ENTRYPOINT [
  "java",
  "--module-path", "mods:lib",
  "--module", "com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main"
]
```

## 22.4 With temporary flags

```dockerfile id="27k9c1"
ENTRYPOINT [
  "java",
  "--add-opens", "com.company.payment.api/com.company.payment.api.request=com.fasterxml.jackson.databind",
  "--module-path", "mods:lib",
  "--module", "com.company.payment.bootstrap/com.company.payment.bootstrap.Main"
]
```

Jika ada ini di Dockerfile, harus ada dokumentasi technical debt.

---

# 23. Kubernetes Implication

Jika menggunakan env var:

```yaml id="sg9944"
env:
  - name: JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS
    value: "--add-opens=com.company.payment.api/com.company.payment.api.request=com.fasterxml.jackson.databind"
```

Catatan praktis: ketika opsi module access dimasukkan lewat environment seperti `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`, bentuk dengan `=` sering lebih aman untuk opsi bernilai, misalnya `--add-opens=module/package=target`. Ada laporan OpenJDK yang menunjukkan perbedaan parsing untuk `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` pada opsi seperti `--add-exports` / `--add-opens`. ([OpenJDK Bugs](https://bugs.openjdk.org/browse/JDK-8320860?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Rekomendasi:

```text id="4ul4o6"
1. Hindari menyebar JPMS flags di banyak Helm values.
2. Centralize flags dalam satu config.
3. Dokumentasikan setiap flag.
4. Jangan biarkan environment-specific flags membuat behavior dev/staging/prod berbeda.
```

---

# 24. Production Readiness Checklist

Sebelum pindah ke module-path production:

| Check | Status |
|---|---|
| Semua internal module punya `module-info.java` yang jelas | Required |
| REST tidak depends persistence | Required |
| Domain bebas Jersey/MyBatis/PostgreSQL | Required |
| DTO serialization/deserialization tested | Required |
| Validation tested | Required |
| MyBatis mapper/entity/typehandler tested | Required |
| Exception mapper/filter/provider tested | Required |
| `jar --describe-module` inventory tersedia | Required |
| Automatic modules diketahui dan diterima | Required |
| Split package dibersihkan | Required |
| `jdeps` report tersedia | Required |
| Docker entrypoint module-path tested | Required |
| Kubernetes manifest updated | Required |
| Temporary `--add-opens` documented | Required |
| Rollback ke classpath runtime tersedia | Strongly recommended |
| Observability/logging works | Required |
| Performance/startup benchmark compared | Recommended |

---

# 25. Go / No-Go Decision

## 25.1 Go for module path if

```text id="ze3gnj"
1. Module-path smoke test stabil.
2. Integration test stabil.
3. Reflection opens minimal dan terdokumentasi.
4. Tidak ada split package.
5. Automatic modules diterima secara sadar.
6. Docker/Kubernetes startup sudah sama dengan CI.
7. Rollback strategy jelas.
```

## 25.2 Stay classpath if

```text id="bwwifp"
1. Banyak dependency belum modular-friendly.
2. Banyak runtime flags diperlukan.
3. Jersey/MyBatis reflection issue belum stabil.
4. CI belum bisa test module-path.
5. Production risk tidak sebanding dengan benefit.
```

Ini bukan kegagalan. Banyak service bisa memakai JPMS untuk **compile-time architecture boundary** sambil tetap menjalankan runtime classpath.


Move to:

```java id="pay4v4"

    exports com.company.payment.api.request;

    opens com.company.payment.api.request to com.fasterxml.jackson.databind;
}
```

# 27. Review Checklist

| Review area | Question |
|---|---|
| Runtime mode | Are we changing classpath/module-path behavior? |
| Docker parity | Does Docker use the same startup command tested in CI? |
| Module descriptor | Is `module-info.java` minimal and explainable? |
| Temporary flags | Are `--add-opens` / `--add-exports` documented? |
| REST registration | Are resources/providers registered explicitly during migration? |
| DTO reflection | Are DTO `opens` tested by integration test? |
| MyBatis reflection | Are mapper/entity/typehandler packages opened only as needed? |
| Split package | Is package ownership clean? |
| Dependency graph | Did new dependency violate layer rules? |
| Rollback | Can we revert runtime mode safely? |

---

# 28. Key Takeaways

```text id="4iok9q"
Classpath is not bad.
Module path is not automatically better.
The right migration path depends on risk, framework behavior, and operational maturity.
```

Untuk Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis enterprise service, target awal yang paling realistis:

```text id="yq9o0q"
JPMS-aware build + clean module boundaries + classpath runtime.
```

Target lanjutan:

```text id="y22v5i"
Module-path runtime only after dependency graph, reflection access,
split packages, Docker entrypoint, and integration tests are stable.
```

Aturan utama:

```text id="x1tc3e"
Do not migrate runtime before you understand the graph.
Do not use --add-opens as design.
Do not open module just to silence framework errors.
Do not claim production JPMS if Docker still runs java -jar.
```
